NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS’ MINUTES 
TUESDAY, August 13, 2013 
 
PRESENT: Sylvia Allen, Supervisor; Dawnafe Whitesinger, Supervisor; David Tenney, Supervisor; Jesse 
Thompson, Supervisor; and Jonathan M. Nez, Chairman were present.  
9:02 a.m.  Call To Order: Pledge of Allegiance and Invocation led by Supervisor Tenney. 
CALL TO THE PUBLIC: There were no public comments.  
CONSENT AGENDA : a) Minutes: July 23, 2013  b) Warrant & Voucher Report over $1000 July 2013  c) 
Constable Reports: Holbrook Precinct #1, June 2013 and Show Low Precinct #5, July 2013  d) Justice Court 
Reports: Holbrook Precinct #1, July 2013; Winslow Precinct #2, July 2013; Snowflake Precinct #3, July 2013; 
Kayenta Precinct #4, July 2013; Show Low Precinct #5, July 2013; and Pinetop -Lakeside Precinct #6, July 
2013 e) Juvenile Probation Report July 2013  f) Clerk of Superior Court Report July 2013  g) Resolution 
Number 22-13, cancelling the election for Silver Creek Flood Protection scheduled for October 2, 2013 and 
deems the position vacant with a four (4) year term to the Board of Directors  h) Conduct Polling Plac e base 
election for Blue Ridge USD #32, Holbrook USD #3, Kayenta USD #27, Show Low USD #10 and Joseph city 
USD #2 on November 5, 2013 to Purpose a Maintenance and Operations question on the ballot i) Sale of 274 
suitcase style voting booths for $1.00 each to Navajo Nation j) High Intensity Drug Trafficking Area (HIDTA) 
Native American Project grant agreement #HT12 -2249 between the City of Tucson and the Sheriff’s Office for 
$1,610 for travel and training k) Agreements for contributions for Senior Centers l) Certify revenue collected by 
Superior Court, Clerk of the Court and all six Justice Courts  m) Tax Exemptions filed pursuant to A.R.S. §42 -
11153 as a Request for Redemption of Waiver for two parcels  n) Name an existing road in the Show 
Low/Linden area to “Perkins Lane” in Section 5, Township 10 North, Range 21 East  o) Modification Number 8 
to Contract Number A11PC00015 Juvenile Detention Services  p) Navajo County comments in response to 50 
CFR Part 17 [Docket No. FWS-R2-ES-2012-0042; 4500030114] RIN 1018-AX13, Endangered and Threatened 
Wildlife and Plants; Designation of Critical Habitat for the Jaguar  q) Letter of support for S. 1300 to amend the 
Healthy Forests Restoration Act of 2003 to provide for the conduct of stewardship end result contracting 
projects r) Congratulatory Letter to the Honorable Ronnie Lupe for the White Mountain Apache Tribe Water 
Rights Agreement : Supervisor Tenney made a motion  to authorize the Chairman to sign the items in the  
consent agenda as presented ; motion seconded by  Supervisor Thompson. Vote unanimous approving the 
motion.  
EMPLOYEE RECOGNITION: Navajo County Recognizes Ivette Williams (Holbrook Justice Court), Laurie 
Kimpell (Probation), Isaura Nez (Elections), Alberto Peshlakai (Public Works), Anna Ericksen (Assesso r), 
Charlotte Yazzie (Juvenile Probation), and James Parks (Information Technology) for their excellence in 
personal performance far exceeding organization expectations : Pam Phillips recogni zed Laurie Kimpell,  Anna 
Ericksen, Charlotte Yazzie and Ivette Wil liams for their excellence and dedication to Navajo County.  Ms. 
Phillips thanked each employee and honored them with a certificate of recognition, a $50 gift card and a leaf to 
put on the employee recognition tree. Anna Ericksen spoke of the Christmas in J uly Back-to-School “Backpack 
Drive.” Mrs. Ericksen indicated this was sponsored by the Care Committee for the Navajo County employee’s 
children. She expressed her appreciation to the Assessor’s office  for their help and to all those who contributed 
to the project.

<!-- page 2 -->
Board of Supervisor Minutes 08-13-2013 
Page 2 of 7 
 
BOARD OF SUPERVISORS:  
a. Presentation to Larry Ponikvar for his heroic act on July 9th: Homero Vela recognized Larry Ponikvar , 
one of Navajo County employees for his quick thinking and quick action in his heroic act of putting his 
life on the line to save  the life of another . Mr. Vela presented Mr. Ponikvar with a plaque which  he 
indicated was a small token  which carries with it  our upmost appreciation, respect and admiration  for 
what he did. Mr. Vela read the plaque which stated; “In appreciation and recognition for Larry Ponikvar, 
who on the 9 th day of July 2013 without thought for his own safety , risked his life to save another by 
pulling an accident victim from a burning car , seconds before it exploded and was totally engulfed in  
flames.” Fire Chief Chavalier from the Show Low Fire Department also recognized Larry Ponikvar.  
Chairman Nez and James Jayne also expressed their admiration and appreciation to Mr. Ponikvar.   
b. Consideration and possible approval of a Proclamation declaring the month of August as “Navajo Code 
Talkers Month” : Eric Descheenie read the proclamation  which credited the Navajo Code Talkers as 
having saved countless lives and helped end World War II by using their Navajo language to transmit 
secret tactical coded messages over military radio to improve communications at both ends in front line 
operation during the war. Mr. Descheenie  discussed the National Navajo Code Talkers D ay which will 
begin tomorrow at 9:00 a.m. in Window Rock, Arizona. Supervisor Thompson made a motion  to 
approve the Proclamation declaring the month of August as Navajo Code Talkers Month; motion 
seconded by Supervisor Tenney . Vote unanimous approving the motion.  Supervisor Allen made 
comments about learning about the Code  Talkers in school and thanked the Board for supporting this 
proclamation.  Chairman Nez and Supervisor Thompson invited the elected officials and everyone to 
the celebration tomorrow and thanked all of the veterans as well. 
c. Consideration and possible approval of a Proclamation declaring the month of August as “Child Support 
Awareness Month”: Brad Carlyon, County Attorney requested the approval of the Proclamation to raise 
awareness for important issues in our Navajo County communities that involve our youth . Supervisor 
Whitesinger made a  motion to approve  the Proclamation declaring the month of August as “Child 
Support Awareness Month”;  motion seconded  by Supervisor Allen . Vote unanimous approving the 
motion.  
d. Introduction of the new Arizona Public Services ’ Cholla Power Plant, Plant Manager Ed Seal : James 
Jayne introduced Brenda Hazlett who represents APS in all of Northern AZ, Steve Quinn, APS Manager 
of the Gila -Navajo Division  who works out of Snowflake  and Ed Seal  who is the new  APS Cholla 
Power Plant Manager. Mr. Ed Seal addressed the Board and spoke about his background and family. Mr. 
Seal indicated he is glad to be the new Cholla Plant Manager and enjoys hi s new role. Supervisor Allen 
welcomed Mr. Seal to Navajo County.  
e. Consideration and possible adoption of Resolution Number 23-13, to oppose the Deregulation of the 
Electric Utility Markets in Arizona: Hunter Moore introduced James Candland whom represents the AZ 
Power Consumers Coalition. Mr. Candland gave a presentation on deregulation and answ ered questions 
of the Board. Supervisor Allen made comments about federal regulations.  Mr. Candland indicated that 
the Navajo Nation President ha s joined the coalition. Supervisor Thompson  and Supervisor 
Whitesinger thanked Mr. Candland. Hunter Moo re read the Resolution  which stated that  it is  the 
opinion of the Navajo County Board of Supervisor that the Arizona Corporation Commission should not 
entertain any action that furthers the deregulation of electric utilities in the State of Arizona . Eric 
Descheenie spoke about the  joint statement of Arizona Speaker of the House Andy Tobin and Speaker 
of the Navajo Nation Council Johnny Naize regarding restructuring of Arizona’s electric m arket. 
Supervisor Allen made a motion to adopt Resolution Number 23 -13, to oppose the Deregulation of 
the Electric Utility Markets in Arizona ; motion seconded by Supervisor Whitesinger. Vote unanimous 
approving the motion.

Board of Supervisor Minutes 08-13-2013 
Page 3 of 7 
 
BOARD OF SUPERVISORS/ASSESSOR : Consideration and possible approval of a settlement 
agreement in the Snowflake Mill Investors, LLC vs  Navajo County in TX2013 -000152: Jason Moore , 
Deputy County Attorney  discussed the item and aske d to convene in to executive session. Cammy Darris, 
Assessor discussed the item further. At 10:09  a.m. Supervisor Tenney made a motion to enter into 
executive session; motion seconded by  Supervisor Allen. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  At 10:09 
a.m. the Board retired into executive session. 
At 10:32 a.m. the Board reconvened.  Supervisor Thompson made a motion to return to regular session ; 
motion seconded by Supervisor Allen. Vote unanimous approving  the motion . Mr. Moore  made a  
recommendation to the Board to approve the settlement proposal  as outlined earlier with the exception that 
the Board not approve paragraph thirteen in  regards to making the settlement proposal  in judgment  
retroactive to June 30, 2013 . Supervisor Tenney made a motion to approve the settlement agreement in 
the Snowflake Mill Investors, LLC vs Navajo County in TX2013 -000152 with the exception of paragraph 
number thirteen in the stipulated agreement ; motion seconded by Supervisor Allen. Vote unanimous 
approving the motion.  
The Board sang Happy Birthday to Supervisor Whitesinger.  
HUMAN RESOURCES: Consideration and possible approval of Personnel Actions : Kimberly Eavenson 
introduced the new Human Re sources Analyst Eric Scott.  M rs. Eavenson reviewed the 20 item personnel 
action report. Supervisor Tenney  made a motion  to approve the personnel action report as presented ; 
motion seconded by Supervisor Allen. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
NAVAJO COUNTY PUBLIC HEALTH SERVICES DISTRICT Boa rd of Directors Session : 
Supervisor Tenney made a motion to enter into the Navajo County Public Health Services District  Board 
of Directors Session ; motion seconded by  Supervisor Whitesinger . Vote unanimous approving the  
motion.  
a. Consideration and possible  approval of a Memorandum of Agreement between Navajo County Public 
Health Services District and Navajo Nation Head Start for a collaboration to provide nutrition and oral 
health services and education to Head Start Staff, children and families in Head Sta rt Centers in the 
Arizona portion of Navajo Nation : Mary Tyler discussed the item. Supervisor Thompson  made a 
motion to approve the  Memorandum of Agreement between Navajo County Public Health Services 
District and Navajo Nation Head Start for a collaboration to provide nutrition and oral health services 
and education to Head Start Staff, children and families in Head Start Centers in the Arizona portion of 
Navajo Nation; motion seconded by Supervisor Tenney. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
b. Consideration and possible approval of a Professional Services Agreement with Connie Baine, RDH, 
AP for Dental Hygiene services funded through  Delta Dental of Arizona Foundation, for the Navajo 
County 1st Grade Fluoride Varnish Program effective August 1, 2013 to May 31, 2014 i n the amount of 
$45.00/per hour: Mary Tyler discussed items b, c, d and e.  
c. Consideration and possible approval of a Pro fessional Services Agreement with Nida Lerch, RDH for 
Dental Hygiene services funded through Delta Dental of Arizona Foundation, for the Navajo County 1 st 
Grade Fluoride Varnish Program effective August 1, 2013 to May 31, 2014 in the amount of $45.00/per 
hour.  
d. Consideration and possible approval of a Professional Services Agreement with Regina Rich, RDH, AP 
for Dental Hygiene services funded through Delta Dental of Arizona Foundation, for the Navajo County 
1st Grade Fluoride Varnish Program effective Augus t 1, 2013 to May 31, 2014 i n the amount of 
$45.00/per hour.

<!-- page 4 -->
Board of Supervisor Minutes 08-13-2013 
Page 4 of 7 
 
e. Consideration and possible approval of a Professional Services Agreement with Vita Nicks, RDH for 
Dental Hygiene services funded through Delta Dental of Arizona Foundation, for the Navajo County 1st 
Grade Fluoride Varnish Program effective August 1, 2013 to May 31, 2014 i n the amount of $45.00/per 
hour: Supervisor Thompson made a motion  to approve items b, c, d, and e; motion seconded by 
Supervisor Allen. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  Supervisor Thompson made a motion to 
return to regular session; motion seconded by  Supervisor Whitesinger. Vote unanimous approving the 
motion.  
TREASURER: Consideration and possible acknowledgement of annual report of collections and 
delinquencies and settlement of accounts : Manny Hernandez discussed the report which included real 
property tax, investments and  the treasurer’s processing fund.  Supervisor Allen  complimented Mr. 
Hernandez and his team  for the work they do . Supervisor Allen made a motion to appro ve the 
acknowledgement of annual report of collections and delinquencies and settlement of accounts ; motion 
seconded by Supervisor Whitesinger. Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
SUPERINTENDENT OF SCHOOLS/FINANCE : Consideration and possible adoption of the fiscal year 
2013-14 property tax rates for all applicable governmental entities within Navajo County : Linda Morrow 
and Tami Phillips discussed the item and answere d question s of the Board. Supervisor Allen made a 
motion to adopt the fiscal year 2013 -14 property tax rates for all applicable governmental entities within 
Navajo County ; motion seconded by  Supervisor Whitesinger. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
James Menlove gave a summary of the Tax Levies & Rates for non-school entities throughout the county. 
Mr. Menlove  indicated this includes tax -rates for Navajo C ounty itself,  Navajo County Library District, 
Navajo County Public Health Service s District, Flood Control Districts and Road Districts. The other 
entities are Fire Districts, Sanitary  Districts, Water Improvement District, Cities and Towns and Northland 
Pioneer Community College. He indicated these entities have their own boards and have ad opted their own 
budgets which they send to the County for their levies and tax rates. Supervisor Tenney made a motion to 
adopt the Navajo County summary of Tax L evies and Rates for Fiscal Year 2013 -14; motion seconded by 
Supervisor Thompson. Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
FINANCE:  
a. Consideration and possible approval of a contract with Tri -Com in the amount of $96,769.50 for CDBG 
project 159-12 Show Low 9 th Street and Hall Street improvements : Mary Springer discussed the item 
and introduced Mr. Shane Hemesath, City E ngineer from the City of Show Low.  She indicated Navajo 
County has partnered with the City of Show Low with the CDBG grant and they are asking approval for 
the contract with Tri Com. Mrs. Springer indicated this will provide handi capped access and to continue 
the pavement at the intersection of 9 th Street and Hall next to the Show Low City Hall and the Show 
Low Public Library . Supervisor Tenney  made a motion to adopt the  contract with Tri -Com in the 
amount of $96,769.50 for CDBG pr oject 159 -12 Show Low 9 th Street and Hall Street improvements ; 
motion seconded by Supervisor Thompson. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
b. Consideration and possible adoption of Resolution Number 24-13, approving up to $10.625 million of 
Pledged Revenue and Revenue Refunding Obligations for the purposes of refunding the 2008 series 
revenue bonds, purchase of security system and equipment for the jail, and construction of a new Public 
Works Complex in Holbrook : James Menlove discussed the item .  Supervisor Thompson made a 
motion to adopt Resolution Number 24-13, approving up to $10.625 million of Pledged Revenue and 
Revenue Refunding Obligations for the purposes of refunding the 2008 series revenue bonds, purchase 
of security system and equipment for the ja il, and construction of a new Public Works Complex in 
Holbrook; motion seconded by Supervisor Tenney. Vote unanimous approving the motion.

<!-- page 5 -->
Board of Supervisor Minutes 08-13-2013 
Page 5 of 7 
 
 
PUBLIC WORKS:  
a. Consideration and possible approval of closure of Territorial road at the Chevelon Creek Bridge for  120 
days due to Bridge Rehabilitation : Homero Vela discussed the item and answered questions of the 
Board. Supervisor Thompson made a motion to approve  the closure  of Territorial road at the 
Chevelon Creek Bridge for 120 days due to Bridge Rehabilitation ; motion seconded by  Supervisor 
Whitesinger. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
b. PUBLIC HEARING: Consideration and possible adoption of Resolution Number 25-13, approving a 
Special Use Permit Amendment to allow Verizon Wireless to extend the height of a wireless 
communication tower, on Assessor’s Parcel Number 208 -19-001E, not to exceed 198 feet : Trent Larson 
discussed the item and answered questions of the Board. Chairman Nez opened the public hearing; Mr. 
Larson indicated that the Planning and Zoning Department did receive one letter in opposition of the 
Special Use Permit. Mr. Larson reported t he Planning and Zoning Commission heard this issue in their 
July 18th meeting and voted unanimously to approve the S pecial Use Permit. Supervisor Allen inquired 
if the extra height of the tower would  improve the service in the area. Kay Hennessy, the representative 
from Verizon Wireless and Pinnacle Consulting indicated that the 15 foot extension to the tower would 
allow Verizon to add an additional microwave anten na to accommodate the new LTE technology that 
will speed up streaming data and better coverage.  Mr. Jayne asked abou t the timing of the project. Ms. 
Hennessy indicated the project is ongoing and should be completed approximately  within the next six 
months. Chairman Nez closed the public hearing. Supervisor Allen made a motion  to adopt 
Resolution Number 25 -13, approving a Special Use Permit Amendment to allow Verizon Wireless to 
extend the height of a wireless communication tower, on Assessor’s Parcel Number 208-19-001E, not to 
exceed 198 feet; motion seconded by Supervisor Tenney. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
c. PUBLIC HEARING: Consideration and possible adoption of Resolution Number 26-13, approving a 
Zone Change from A -General to Commercial -Residential on Assessor’s Parcel Number 103 -30-079 
(Trent Larson) Item Pulled.  
COUNTY BUSINESS UPDATE : Report from Board members, County Attorney and County Manager : 
Supervisor Whitesinger reported she attended the Summit Health Care Town Hall m eeting to see some of 
the renovations that have been happening at  Summit Health Care . She indicated she attended the Pinetop 
Town Council meeting where she  spoke on the  completion of the multi -use path and the upcoming 
partnership with Chief Ron Wheeler, the new Pinetop-Lakeside Police Chief. She reported on the graduation 
of the Whi te Mountain Youth Corp s where many of the graduates  spoke of the  positive impact that the 
Youth Core has on them. Supervisor Whitesinger spoke of the First Things First Board meeting and City 
4’s County Connections program. She indicated Sheriff Clark and Mr. Carlyon invited her to be a m ediator 
for their town hall and stated she also had the opportunity to speak to survivors at the suicide prevention 
conference. Supervisor Allen invited everyone to the Snowflake Taylor Youth  Football League Kickoff on 
Saturday at 8:00 a.m . at the Snowflake High School. She expressed appreciation  to the county team and 
employee’s for their efforts at the Cedar Hills clean-up day which she expres sed was a huge success. She 
indicated that there were many from the Cedar Hill s area who expressed their thanks. She reported she 
watched a movie called “Sick and Sicker” that American Prosperity put on which was a documentary on 
Canada’s healthcare. Supervisor Allen  spoke in regards to the  proposed critical habitat under the  
endangered species act; a water snake,  cactus, jumping mouse, jaguar, wolf and many more which will 
significantly impact the State in the way we use our  forests, lakes and public lands. She then discussed the 
AZ-NM C oalition of C ounties meeting where they conversed about  the endangered species and predator 
issues and how counties can join  together to write an agreement to present to the U.S. Fish and Wildlife

<!-- page 6 -->
Board of Supervisor Minutes 08-13-2013 
Page 6 of 7 
 
Service. Supervisor Tenney reported on meetings in Colville, Washington -Oregon where he visited the 
Vaagen Brothers Lumber saw m ill. He indicated they also run a small mill in Eager , AZ and they hope to 
become involved in  the 2 nd phase of 4FRI . Supervisor Tenney  spoke of several issues regarding 4FRI 
which is working  to restore our forest s. He expressed concerns about the Pioneer contract with the Forest 
Service which is trying to transfer the contract to an undisclosed company. Supervisor Tenney indicated he 
has been visi ting with and  getting support from the  congressional legislation; the Kirkpatrick staff, the  
McCain staff, also in DC with Congressman Gosar and Senator  Flake to carry the message to  influence the 
Forest Service to be more diligent this time in making sure the contract will be awarded to a company who 
can execute and restore our forests. He will be meeting with Cal Joyner, the new Regional Forest Supervisor 
in Albuquerque to help foster their  relationship. He indicated he is a football coach in Show Low and 
reported his son went to serve  a church mission in Johannesburg, South Africa . Supervisor Thompson  
reported on the County and Tribal Government Relationships Subcommittee meeting he serves on at the 
NACo Annual Conference in Texas where they discussed the relationship and communication between the 
tribes and the federal government.  The Tribal sub -committee indicated they would like to have more 
meetings with the federal officials in attendance. He reported he met Alberto Peshlakai’s great grandmother 
whom he told of Mr. Peshlakai’s excell ent service and  valuable contributions to Navajo C ounty and its  
communities. Supervisor Thompson reported he was  honored to have been asked to be the M aster o f 
Ceremonies at the Chief Manuelito Scholarship Awards Ceremony  for 113 young scholars. He reported he 
attended the Annual Birdsprings Senior Center Opening Celebration. He then discussed the NACOG TPAC 
meeting where he has been asked to continue to serve as a Navajo C ounty member of the TPAC committee 
as a tribal representative for the 2013 -2015 membership appointments. He reported they were updated on 
State and Federal transportation issues  Supervisor Thompson spoke of his meeting with Congresswoman  
Ann Kirkpatrick regarding transportation on the reservation roads and the problems the BLM is having with 
wild horses in their land areas. Chairman Nez reported on District I. He indicated an emergency declaration 
of drought was signed by the  Navajo Nation P resident because of the monsoon damage to roads from the 
recent flooding. He expressed appreciation to  Supervisor Allen , Homero Vela , Sheriff Clark and county 
constituents for attending the Navajo Nation Council summer session. Chairman Nez  stated he went to 
Hawaii to an event for the Navajo Nation. Chairman Nez  invited the people of Navajo  County to the 
Navajo Code Talker event in Window Rock  at the Veteran’s Memorial Park tomorrow,  August 14 th. Brad 
Carlyon, County Attorney reported on the White Mountain Sheriff’s  Posse Annual BBQ fundraiser which 
was well attended. He indicated last Friday he and Sheriff Clark had a Criminal Justice Town Hall in Show 
Low where Supervisor Whitesinger and Hunter Moore acted as moderators. Mr. Carlyon reported they met 
with law enforcement and attorneys with the White Mountain Apache Tribe to discuss a possible agreement 
to house tribal  inmates in our jail facility and also had discussions to finalize the law enforcement cross 
commission agreement. He indicated t hey are working on two  agreements regarding those issues  for the 
Board to discuss and possibly vote upon at a future Board of Supervisors meeting.  Mr. Carlyon spoke about 
the Family Advocacy Center’s fashion show fundraiser which he indicated was very successful. He reported 
the Annual Arizona Prosecuting Attorneys Advisory Council Summer Conference was held in Tucson. He 
commended Joel Ruechel who was given a Lifetime Achievement A ward for his dedication to protecting 
our communities . He indicated  next week is the AACo Summer Conference in the Valley . Mr. Jayne 
reported on Family Advocacy Center fundraiser event and discussed attending the Sequoia Village School 
ribbon cutting which he indicated was a beautiful facility.  
Navajo Word of the Day: Siláołtsooí - meaning military veteran. 
ADJOURN: At 12:04 p.m.  Supervisor Tenney made a motion to adjourn ; motion seconded by  
Supervisor Whitesinger. Vote unanimous approving the motion.
